Tensorial time derivatives on moving surfaces: General concepts and a specific application for surface Landau-de Gennes models Journal: Journal of Geometry and Physics
Ingo Nitschke, Axel Voigt

Abstract
Observer-invariance is regarded as a minimum requirement for an appropriate definition of time derivatives. We systematically discuss such time derivatives for surface tensor field and provide explicit formulations for material, upper-convected, lower-convected and Jaumann/corotational time derivatives which all lead to different physical implications. We compare these results with the corresponding time derivatives for tangential tensor fields. As specific surface 2-tensor fields we consider surface Q-tensor fields and conforming surface Q-tensor fields and apply the results in surface Landau-de Gennes models for surface liquid crystals.



2020 MSC
53A45 , 53A05 , 37C10 , 70G45

1
Introduction

Observer-invariant time derivatives for tensor-fields on moving surfaces
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
are important ingredients for various applications, such as fluid deformable surfaces and surface liquid crystals, see, e.g.,
[
1
,
2
,
3
,
4
,
5
]
. They determine specific rates of change independently of their observation and specify transport mechanism reflecting a certain inertia in the considered quantity induced by material motions. For tangential tensor-fields, defined in the tangent bundle of
\mathcal{S}
, denoted by
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
, time derivatives for arbitrary observer are discussed in detail in
[
6
]
. Unlike for scalar fields, where the time derivative is uniquely defined, severe differences in the evolution of the tangential tensor-field, e.g. a surface director field or a surface Q-tensor field in surface liquid crystal models
[
7
]
, have been identified. The implications of these differences, e.g. in morphogenesis
[
8
,
9
,
10
]
, which can be modelled using fluid deformable surfaces and surface liquid crystals are not yet explored. The requirement of the surface tensor-fields to be tangential might be too strong for such applications. They require surface tensor-fields with an additional normal component, see, e.g.,
[
11
]
for a director field on a flexible membrane and
[
12
,
13
,
14
,
15
]
for surface Q-tensor-fields but on stationary surfaces. These surface tensor-fields are defined in
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
, and need slightly different time derivatives, which respect the embedding space
\mathbb{R}^{3}
as well as the surface
\mathcal{S}
. We will systematically discuss these time derivatives, their properties and relations and apply them to a surface Landau-de Gennes model.

Unlike in
[
6
]
, we do not take a spacetime manifold as a basis for observer-invariant time derivatives. The advantage is an improved readability due to lesser abstract concepts. The disadvantage is that we do not get any longer observer-invariance for free as a results of a covariance principle w. r. t. the choice of spacetime coordinates. The main issue to develop observer-invariant time derivatives is that the time
t
is not a coordinate of
\mathcal{S}
, but rather a parameter to describe time-dependencies w. r. t. an observer and the relation between time and space. We need to demonstrate that the time derivatives of tensor-fields on moving surfaces are invariant within the observer class depicting the moving surface. In parts we circumvent this issue by stipulating time derivatives for a material observer (Lagrange perspective) and transform these representation to an arbitrary observer. We only consider instantaneous tensor-fields.

We introduce notation in subsection
1.1
and provide a short tabular summary in subsection
1.2
. The actual derivation of time derivatives is constituted in section
2
, which is organized in the following way.
Subsection
2.1
describes the general approach to obtain time derivatives.
Basically, we use a differential quotient w. r. t. the time parameter
t
s. t. a time derivative yields a certain rate of the considered tensor-fields.
Such an approach is only sufficient for fixed choices of convenient pullbacks, which are capable of evaluating a “future” tensor-field on the current surface. We illustrate this approach for scalar fields in
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
, where such a pullback seems to be uniquely given.
The situation changes for
n
-tensor fields with
n\geq 1
, where different pullbacks lead to different time derivatives.
In subsection
2.2
we derive the material time derivative,
in subsection
2.3
the upper-convected time derivative,
in subsection
2.4
the lower-convected time derivative
and in subsection
2.5
the Jaumann/corotational time derivative.
The individual derivatives are build on each other.
In all of these subsections we also consider vector- as well as 2-tensor-fields separately for the sake of readability. Additionally, we show at the end of each subsection that all of these time derivatives are thin-film limits of usual flat
\mathbb{R}^{3}
time derivatives. With surface Landau-de Gennes models for surface liquid crystals in mind
[
14
]
, we treat in
subsection
2.6
Q-tensor fields in
\mathchoice{\operatorname{Q}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{Q}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{Q}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{Q}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
, which are symmetric and trace-free, as a special case of surface 2-tensor fields.
Here we consider only the material and Jaumann/corotational time derivative.
Moreover, we discuss surface conforming Q-tensor fields in
\mathchoice{\operatorname{C_{\mathcal{S}}Q}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{C_{\mathcal{S}}Q}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{C_{\mathcal{S}}Q}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{C_{\mathcal{S}}Q}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
, where the eigenvector spaces are aligned to the surface.
This gives the opportunity to modify the material derivative to a simpler representation. Using these tools we formulate surface Landau-de Gennes models on evolving surfaces which lead for the material time derivative to the same formulation as postulated in
[
14
]
.

1.1
Notation

adjusted surface gradient of
\boldsymbol{V}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{A}}[\boldsymbol{V}]=\frac{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{G}}[\boldsymbol{V}]-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{G}}^{T}[\boldsymbol{V}]}{2}

antisymmetric part of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{G}}[\boldsymbol{V}]

Table 2:
Some frequently used quantities. Note that
\boldsymbol{V}
is used as a placeholder for
\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}
and
\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}
in case the observer choice is relevant.

We mainly adopt notations from
[
7
]
.
Nevertheless, we give a condensed summary in this section including some notational extensions.
A moving surface
\mathcal{S}
is sufficiently described by parameterizations

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{X}:\quad\mathcal{T}\times\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}:\quad(t,y^{1},y^{2})\mapsto\boldsymbol{X}(t,y^{1},y^{2})\in\mathcal{S}|_{t}\,\text{,}

(1)

where
\mathcal{U}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
is the chart codomain and
\mathcal{T}=[t_{0},t_{1}]\subset\mathbb{R}
the time domain.
For simplicity we assume that
\boldsymbol{X}(t,\mathcal{U})=\mathcal{S}|_{t}
can be achieved by a single time-depending parameterization
\boldsymbol{X}
for all
t\in\mathcal{T}
.
The results can be extended to the more general case considering subsets providing an open covering of
\mathcal{S}
.
We omit the time parameter
t
in the notation if it is clear that the considered term can be evaluated temporally locally.
A parameterization is not uniquely given for a moving surface.
For instance comprises
\boldsymbol{X}
information about the observer. Due to this, we subscribe quantities with
{\!\mathfrak{m}}
if we consider the material observer and

{\!\mathfrak{o}}
if we consider an arbitrary observer, see
[
6
]
for more details about observer.
One could refer the material observer to the Lagrangian perspective/specification.
Since we also consider motion in normal direction of the surface and the observer has to follow the material in this direction,
a pure Eulerian perspective does not exist on moving surfaces generally.
Note that we assume that
\boldsymbol{X}
provides a sufficiently smooth embedding of
\mathcal{S}
into
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

We write
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
as a shorthand for the space of sufficiently smooth
n
-tensor field on
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
i. e. for
\boldsymbol{R}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
and
(y^{1},y^{2})\in\mathcal{U}
the quantity
\boldsymbol{R}(y^{1},y^{2})\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{{\boldsymbol{X}(y^{1},y^{2})}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{{\boldsymbol{X}(y^{1},y^{2})}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{{\boldsymbol{X}(y^{1},y^{2})}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{{\boldsymbol{X}(y^{1},y^{2})}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}\cong(\mathbb{R}^{3})^{n}

is a usual
\mathbb{R}^{3}
-
n
-tensor defined at
\boldsymbol{X}(y^{1},y^{2})\in\mathcal{S}
.
This means that we handle tensor bundles and fields (section of bundles) synonymously due to the assumed smooth structure.
We also does not distinguish between co- and contravariant tensor fields, and everything between, in index-free notations, since
they are isomorph by the musical isomorphisms (
\flat
,
\sharp
) for a given metric and all operators used in this paper respect that.
The space of tangential
n
-tensor fields
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
is a subtensor field of
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
,
i. e. it holds the subtensor relation
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{{\boldsymbol{X}(y^{1},y^{2})}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{{\boldsymbol{X}(y^{1},y^{2})}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{{\boldsymbol{X}(y^{1},y^{2})}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{{\boldsymbol{X}(y^{1},y^{2})}}}}\mathcal{S}<\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{{\boldsymbol{X}(y^{1},y^{2})}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{{\boldsymbol{X}(y^{1},y^{2})}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{{\boldsymbol{X}(y^{1},y^{2})}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{{\boldsymbol{X}(y^{1},y^{2})}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}

for all
(y^{1},y^{2})\in\mathcal{U}
.
The space
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
contains only the fields from
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
that can be represented by a tangential frame.
We summarize the most used subtensor fields of
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
for
n\in{0,1,2}
in this paper in table
1
.
Some of them are defined in their associated section, where they are used.
For
n=1
we omit the index, e. g. it is
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{1}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{1}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{1}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{1}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
.
Every subtensor field relation brings its uniquely defined orthogonal projection
\operatorname{\Pi}_{(\cdot)}
along,
which is labeled by its image, i. e. the subtensor field space.
The orthogonal projection
\operatorname{\Pi}_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.40399pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.40399pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.40399pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.40399pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.40399pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.40399pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.40399pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.40399pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}}:\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
projects
n
-tensor fields into tangential
n
-tensor fields for instance.
We use the global Cartesian as well as local tangential frames and thus, for a better readability, also different index notations (Ricci calculus) in accordance with their frame.
We apply capital Latin letters
A,B,C,\ldots
w. r. t. the Cartesian frame
\{\boldsymbol{e}_{A}\}
,
e. g. we could use
R^{AB}\boldsymbol{e}_{A}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{B}
to describe a 2-tensor field
\boldsymbol{R}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
.
Small Latin letters
i,j,k,\ldots
are used w. r. t. the tangential frame
\{\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{X}\}
derived from parameterization (
1
).
For instance we could write
r^{ij}\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{X}\otimes\partial_{j}\boldsymbol{X}
for a tangential 2-tensor field
\boldsymbol{r}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
.

We only use two kinds of spatial derivatives.
One is the covariant derivative
\nabla:\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+1}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.20226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.57304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+1}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.20226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.57304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+1}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.20226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.57304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+1}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.20226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.57304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
defined by the Christoffel symbols

\Gamma_{ijk}=\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{i}g_{jk}+\partial_{j}g_{ik}-\partial_{k}g_{ij})
in a usual way,
where
g_{ij}
is the covariant proxy of the metric tensor.
In index notations, we represent
\nabla
with a stroke “
|
”.
For instance, we write
\mathchoice{[\nabla\boldsymbol{r}]^{{{ij}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}}{[\nabla\boldsymbol{r}]^{{{ij}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}}{[\nabla\boldsymbol{r}]^{{{ij}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}}{[\nabla\boldsymbol{r}]^{{{ij}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}}=\mathchoice{r^{{{ij}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.61615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.61615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06631pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.9045pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{|k}}}}{r^{{{ij}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.61615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.61615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06631pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.9045pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{|k}}}}{r^{{{ij}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.61615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.61615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06631pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.9045pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{|k}}}}{r^{{{ij}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.61615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.61615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.06631pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.9045pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{|k}}}}=\partial_{k}r^{ij}+\Gamma_{kl}^{i}r^{lj}+\Gamma_{kl}^{j}r^{il}

for
\boldsymbol{r}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
.
The other one is the surface derivative
\operatorname{\nabla}_{\mathrm{C}}:\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}\otimes\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}<\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+1}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.20226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.57304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+1}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.20226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.57304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+1}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.20226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.57304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+1}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.20226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.57304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}

defined as the covariant derivative on the Cartesian proxy components, which are scalar fields in
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
.
As an example, it is
\operatorname{\nabla}_{\mathrm{C}}\boldsymbol{R}=\boldsymbol{e}_{A}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{B}\otimes\nabla R^{AB}
valid for
\boldsymbol{R}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
.
For readers from other communities, it holds
\operatorname{\nabla}_{\mathrm{C}}\boldsymbol{R}=(\operatorname{\widehat{\nabla}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}})|_{\mathcal{S}}\boldsymbol{Id}_{\mathcal{S}}
,
where
\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}
is an arbitrary smooth extension s. t.
\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}|_{\mathcal{S}}=\boldsymbol{R}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
is valid,

\operatorname{\widehat{\nabla}}:\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+1}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.20226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.57304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+1}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.20226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.57304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+1}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.20226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.57304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+1}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.71034pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.20226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.57304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}
the usual
\mathbb{R}^{3}
-gradient
and
\boldsymbol{Id}_{\mathcal{S}}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
the surface identity, resp. tangential projection, tensor field,
e. g. given by
[\boldsymbol{Id}_{\mathcal{S}}]^{AB}=\delta^{AB}-\nu^{A}\nu^{B}
or
[\boldsymbol{Id}_{\mathcal{S}}]^{ij}=g^{ij}
.
Both derivatives are also related outside the Cartesian frame and we give these relations in the appropriated locations in this paper where they are needed.
Further definitions for covariant differential operators like
\operatorname{div}
(divergence),
\operatorname{rot}
(curl),
\Delta
(Bochner-Laplace), etc.,
are derived from
\nabla
in the usual way.
In the example section
2.6.3
we use the surface Laplace operator
\operatorname{\Delta}_{\mathrm{C}}:=(\operatorname{Tr}\operatorname{\nabla}_{\mathrm{C}}^{2}):\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
on 2-tensor fields,
which stated a kinda connection Laplace operator a priori w. r. t. surface derivative
\operatorname{\nabla}_{\mathrm{C}}
.
For more details see lemmas
24
and
25
,
where we show that
\operatorname{\Delta}_{\mathrm{C}}
is the Laplace-Beltrami operator on the Cartesian Proxy components as well as a Bochner-like Laplace operator w. r. t.
\operatorname{\nabla}_{\mathrm{C}}
.
Additionally, corollary
26
gives a relation to covariant differential operators outside the Cartesian frame.
Inner products
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle_{(\cdot)}
are written with angle brackets and labeled by its associated space.
For instance
\left\langle\boldsymbol{r}_{1},\boldsymbol{r}_{2}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}}=g_{ik}g_{jl}r_{1}^{ij}r_{2}^{kl}
is the local inner product,
or
\left\langle\boldsymbol{r}_{1},\boldsymbol{r}_{2}\right\rangle_{\operatorname{L}^{\!2}(\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S})}=\int_{\mathcal{S}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{r}_{1},\boldsymbol{r}_{2}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}}\operatorname{d}\mathcal{S}
is the global inner product
of
\boldsymbol{r}_{1},\boldsymbol{r}_{2}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
.
Note that inner products on tensor fields are backwards compatible with their subtensor fields, e. g. it holds

\left\langle\boldsymbol{r}_{1},\boldsymbol{r}_{2}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}=\left\langle\boldsymbol{r}_{1},\boldsymbol{r}_{2}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}}
for
\boldsymbol{r}_{1},\boldsymbol{r}_{2}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
.
Norms are given and written according to their inner products, e. g. it is
\left\|\boldsymbol{r}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}}^{2}=\left\langle\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{r}\right\rangle_{\operatorname{L}^{\!2}(\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S})}
valid for
\boldsymbol{r}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}
.
We save writing an extra operation symbol, like a dot, for simple tensor-tensor multiplications
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{m}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.30226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.07304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{m}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.30226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.07304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{m}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.30226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.07304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{m}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.30226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.07304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+m-2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.14288pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.14288pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[15.13785pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.81274pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+m-2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.14288pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.14288pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[15.13785pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.81274pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+m-2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.14288pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.14288pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[15.13785pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.81274pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+m-2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.14288pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.14288pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[15.13785pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.81274pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
,
e. g. it is
\boldsymbol{R}_{1}\boldsymbol{R}_{2}=R_{1}^{AB}R_{2B}\boldsymbol{e}_{A}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
valid for
\boldsymbol{R}_{1}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
and
\boldsymbol{R}_{2}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
.
However, we sometimes use the double-dot symbol “
:
” for the double-contraction product
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{m}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.30226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.07304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{m}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.30226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.07304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{m}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.30226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.07304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{m}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.09612pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.30226pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.07304pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+m-4}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.14288pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.14288pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[15.13785pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.81274pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+m-4}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.14288pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.14288pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[15.13785pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.81274pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+m-4}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.14288pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.14288pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[15.13785pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.81274pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n+m-4}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.14288pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.14288pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[15.13785pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.81274pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
,
e. g. it holds
\boldsymbol{R}_{1}\operatorname{:}\boldsymbol{R}_{2}=\left\langle\boldsymbol{R}_{1},\boldsymbol{R}_{2}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
for
\boldsymbol{R}_{1},\boldsymbol{R}_{2}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
.
As in
[
7
]
, we use arguments in square brackets to denote functional dependencies,
e. g. the scalar field

f[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]=\left\|\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}\right\|_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}^{2}+\left\|\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}^{2}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}

depends on the material surface parameterization
\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}
as a proxy for the surface, w. r. t. the material observer, as well as on its velocity
\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}=\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}
.
Note that functional dependencies do not have to be mutual independent as we see in the former example.
In table
2
frequently used quantities, also related to the chosen observer, are summarized.
We would also like to point out that
A
contains a collection of lemmas, corollaries and their justifications that may be helpful
for understanding the quantities in table
2
.
For more details on observer related notations, see
[
6
]
.
Note that for the tangential material derivative, which is defined below, we use a dot over the field symbol.
A bar between a term and the dot parenthesize the term under the dot.
For instance, we write
\dot{f}=\dot{\overline{f_{1}f_{2}}}
for
f=f_{1}f_{2}
in context of scalar fields.

1.2
Summary

In this section we provide a summary of the results in section
2
and relate them to the observer-invariant time derivatives derived in
[
6
]
.
Tables
4
and
6
give an overview about tangential time derivatives
on tangential vector fields in
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}
and 2-tensor fields in
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
, which are given in
[
6
]
.
We formulate these time derivatives w. r. t. an observer parameterization as well as a relation to the material time derivative.
Note that these time derivatives are special cases of instantaneous tensor fields in
[
6
]
.
We use the name prefix “Jaumann” synonymously to the prefix “corotational”.
Time derivatives on vector fields in
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
and 2-tensor fields in
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
, which are derived in section
2
, are summarized in
table
4
and
6
.
We formulate these time derivatives in an orthogonal tangential-normal-decomposition for tangential-normal-decomposed tensor fields,
where we are able to use the corresponding tangential time-derivatives.
This representation could be useful for analytical perspectives.
In contrast, we give also a relation to the material derivative, which could be helpful for numerical implementations,
since it is possible to apply a Cartesian frame of the embedding space for the material derivative,
i. e.
\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}=\dot{R}^{A}\boldsymbol{e}_{A}
for vector fields
\boldsymbol{R}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
and
\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}=\dot{R}^{AB}\boldsymbol{e}_{A}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{B}
for 2-tensor fields
\boldsymbol{R}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
,
see (
8
) for general
n
-tensor fields.
A very useful property of the material and Jaumann derivative is their inner product compatibility,
i. e. the inner product of tensor fields obey the product rule, see corollaries

2
,
11
for vector fields and
4
,
13

for 2-tensor fields.
Likewise, they yield a compatible product rule with the tensor-vector product
\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
,
see corollaries
5
and
14
.
Both convected derivatives do not exhibit these behaviors.
Note that the material derivative is not an extension of the tangential material derivative, contrary to all other time derivatives we present in this paper.
However, the pure tangential part of the material derivative yields such an extension,
which in turn describes an extension of
[
6
, Proposition 4]
for non-tangential tensor fields.
