2
Derivations

2.1
General Approach and Scalar Fields

Formally, we could define an arbitrary time-derivative on
\boldsymbol{R}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
by

\displaystyle(\operatorname{D}_{t}\boldsymbol{R})[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})

\displaystyle:=\lim_{\tau\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{\tau}\left((\Phi^{*}_{t,\tau}\boldsymbol{R}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]|_{t+\tau})(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})-\boldsymbol{R}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})\right)

(2)

where

\Phi^{*}_{t,\tau}:\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}^{n}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S},t+\tau}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}^{n}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S},t}

is a convenient pullback by the map

\displaystyle\Phi_{t,\tau}:\mathcal{S}|_{t}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}|_{t+\tau}:\quad\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})\mapsto\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}(t+\tau,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})\,\text{.}

Even if the time derivative is described by a material observer, w. r. t. its parameterization
\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}
,
we are able to evaluate (
2
) by an arbitrary observer, w. r. t. parameterization
\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}
with the aid of
relation

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{R}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})=\boldsymbol{R}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}](t,(\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}|_{t}^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}})(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2}))\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}^{n}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}\,\text{,}

(3)

respectively, the inverse relation

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{R}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}](t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{2})=\boldsymbol{R}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t,(\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}|_{t}^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}})(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{2}))\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}^{n}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{2})}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}\,\text{.}

The general proceeding is to assume a pullback, conclude the associated time derivative w. r. t. the material observer and transform it w. r. t. an arbitrary observer to establish observer-invariance.

For scalar fields
f\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}=\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
, i. e.
n=0
, the only noteworthy pullback is simply given by

\displaystyle(\Phi^{*_{0}}_{t,\tau}f[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]|_{t+\tau})(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})

\displaystyle=f[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t+\tau,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}^{0}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})}\mathcal{S}\,\text{.}

(4)

Hence, with
\dot{f}:=\operatorname{D}_{t}|_{\Phi^{*}_{t,\tau}=\Phi^{*_{0}}_{t,\tau}}f
, (
2
) becomes

\displaystyle\dot{f}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}f[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}^{0}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})}\mathcal{S}

for a material observer.
Getting the time derivative
\dot{f}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}]
for an arbitrary observer given by parameterization
\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}
is more difficult.
The time derivative (
2
) as well as the pullback (
4
) have to be evaluated w. r. t. the relation (
3
).
As we can see in appendix
B.1
, applying a Taylor expansion to this pullback at
\tau=0
leads to

\displaystyle\dot{f}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}](t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{2})

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}f[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}](t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{2})+(\nabla_{\boldsymbol{u}}f)[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}](t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{2})\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}^{0}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{2})}\mathcal{S}\,\text{,}

\displaystyle\text{where}\quad\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{u}[\boldsymbol{X}_{\!\mathfrak{o}},\boldsymbol{X}_{\!\mathfrak{m}}](t,y^{1}_{\!\mathfrak{o}},y^{1}_{\!\mathfrak{o}})

\displaystyle:=\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}[\boldsymbol{X}_{\!\mathfrak{m}}](t,(\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}|_{t}^{-1}\circ\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}})(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{2}))-\boldsymbol{V}_{\!\mathfrak{o}}[\boldsymbol{X}_{\!\mathfrak{o}}](t,y^{1}_{\!\mathfrak{o}},y^{1}_{\!\mathfrak{o}})

is the relative velocity,

\boldsymbol{V}_{\!\mathfrak{o}}[\boldsymbol{X}_{\!\mathfrak{o}}](t,y^{1}_{\!\mathfrak{o}},y^{1}_{\!\mathfrak{o}}):=\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}(t,y^{1}_{\!\mathfrak{o}},y^{1}_{\!\mathfrak{o}})
the observer velocity
and
\boldsymbol{V}_{\!\mathfrak{m}}[\boldsymbol{X}_{\!\mathfrak{m}}](t,y^{1}_{\!\mathfrak{m}},y^{1}_{\!\mathfrak{m}}):=\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}(t,y^{1}_{\!\mathfrak{m}},y^{1}_{\!\mathfrak{m}})
the material velocity.
This is also consistent with the scalar-valued time derivative given in
[
6
]
.
Since the observer is arbitrary and for sake of simplicity, we also write

\displaystyle\dot{f}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}f+\nabla_{\boldsymbol{u}}f\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}

(5)

for short, which is the common form in context of non(-Einstein)-relativistic settings
[
16
]
,
and ALE (Arbitrary Lagrangian–Eulerian) methods on non-stationary surfaces
[
17
,
18
]
.
A material perspective, i. e.
\boldsymbol{u}=0
, applies to Lagrangian particle methods
[
19
,
20
]
for instance.
If
f
is extended in a volume around
\mathcal{S}
, there are also alternative formulation of
\dot{f}
,
see
[
21
]
for instance.

Since we consider
\mathbb{R}^{3}
quantities, even though restricted to the surface, we show at the end of each of the following subsections that all
considered time derivatives are consistent to their counterpart in a volume,
i. e. the thin film limit of a time derivative in a bulk equals its time derivatives on the surface.
We use the thin film parameterization
\boldsymbol{\chi}[\boldsymbol{X}]
, defined by

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\chi}[\boldsymbol{X}](t,y^{1},y^{2},\xi)

\displaystyle:=\boldsymbol{X}(t,y^{1},y^{2})+\xi\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}](t,y^{1},y^{2})

(6)

with
\xi\in[-h,h]
,
to describe the thin film
\mathcal{S}_{h}
around
\mathcal{S}
, see
[
13
]
for more details.
Therefore,
\boldsymbol{\chi}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]
is the material and
\boldsymbol{\chi}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}]
an arbitrary observer thin film parameterization.
According to this,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}:=\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\chi}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]
is the material and

\widehat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}:=\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\chi}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}]
the observer thin film velocity.
We obtain the relative thin film velocity

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{u}-\xi\boldsymbol{I\!I}\boldsymbol{u}

as a consequence by (
53
).
For extended scalar fields
\widehat{f}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}_{h}
, which are sufficing
\widehat{f}|_{\xi=0}=f\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
,
we use the Taylor expansion

\displaystyle\widehat{f}

\displaystyle=f+\xi(\partial_{\xi}\widehat{f})|_{\xi=0}+\mathcal{O}(\xi^{2})

at
\xi=0
.
Note that the normal coordinate
\xi
and the time parameter
t
are mutually independent,
i. e.
\partial_{\xi}
and
\partial_{t}
are commuting on scalar fields.
Eventually, this yields

\displaystyle\dot{\widehat{f}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\widehat{f}+\operatorname{\widehat{\nabla}}_{\widehat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}}\widehat{f}=\dot{f}+\mathcal{O}(\xi)\,\text{,}

(7)

i. e. it holds
\dot{\widehat{f}}\rightarrow\dot{f}
for
h\rightarrow 0
.

2.2
Material Derivative

In order to obtain the material time derivative we could simply use the Cartesian frame
\{\boldsymbol{e}_{A}\}
, which is Eulerian and constant in space.
Though it seems that an additional frame, which is not given by the chart through the parameterization, would complicate the situation at first glance,
the material pullback becomes quite easy.
The pullback implements the scalar pullback (
4
) on each Cartesian component.
This yields the definition

\displaystyle(\Phi^{*_{m}}_{t,\tau}\boldsymbol{R}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]|_{t+\tau})(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})

\displaystyle:=R^{A_{1}\ldots A_{n}}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t+\tau,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})\bigotimes_{\alpha=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{A_{\alpha}}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}^{n}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}\,\text{.}

Therefore the material derivative is given by

\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}:=\operatorname{D}_{t}|_{\Phi^{*}_{t,\tau}=\Phi^{*_{m}}_{t,\tau}}
, i. e.

\displaystyle(\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R})[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}R^{A_{1}\ldots A_{n}}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})\bigotimes_{\alpha=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{A_{\alpha}}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}^{n}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}\,\text{,}

for the material observer.
Since the frame is constant, we only have to consider the scalar Cartesian proxy fields
R^{A_{1}\ldots A_{n}}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
.
For an arbitrary observer, (
5
) yields

\displaystyle\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}=\dot{R}^{A_{1}\ldots A_{n}}\bigotimes_{\alpha=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{A_{\alpha}}\,\text{.}

(8)

One first observation of (
8
) is that this time derivative equals the material time-derivative in a volume up to the restriction to the surface,
i. e. it does not depend on behaviors of the surface at all.
This is not to be expected by other time-derivatives.
Moreover, (
8
), contrary to (
2
) in general, is now represented in context of an arbitrary observer chart,
i. e. all we have to do is calculating (
8
) also in terms of an arbitrary extended surface observer frame

\{\partial_{1}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}},\partial_{2}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}},\boldsymbol{\nu}\}
.
Note that the Cartesian frame
yields

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{e}_{A}

\displaystyle=\delta_{AB}\left(g_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{ij}\partial_{j}X_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{B}\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{X}_{\!\mathfrak{o}}+\nu^{B}\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\,\text{,}

at all local events
(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{2})
.
In the following subsections we transform the frame and the associated proxy fields to the extended observer frame especially for vector and 2-tensor fields.

For extended tensor fields
\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}_{h}
, which are sufficing
\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}|_{\xi=0}=\boldsymbol{R}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
,
we conclude from (
8
) and (
7
) that

\displaystyle\dot{\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}}

\displaystyle=\dot{\widehat{R}}^{A_{1}\ldots A_{n}}\bigotimes_{\alpha=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{A_{\alpha}}\rightarrow\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}

(9)

is valid for
h\rightarrow 0
.

2.2.1
Vector Fields

To represent the material derivative
\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}=\dot{R}^{A}\boldsymbol{e}_{A}
(
8
) on vector fields
\boldsymbol{R}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
,
we use the orthogonal decomposition

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{R}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{r}+\phi\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}\,\text{,}

(10)

where
\boldsymbol{r}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}
and
\phi\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
are given by
\boldsymbol{R}
uniquely.
The tangential covariant observer proxy of
\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}
yields

\displaystyle\left\langle\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R},\partial_{k}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{AB}\dot{R}^{A}\partial_{k}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{B}=\dot{\overline{\left\langle\boldsymbol{R},\partial_{k}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}\right\rangle}}_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}-R_{B}\dot{\overline{\partial_{k}X_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{B}}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}r_{k}+u^{i}\partial_{i}r_{k}-\left(r_{B}+\phi\nu_{B}\right)\left(\partial_{k}V_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{B}+u^{i}\partial_{i}\partial_{k}X_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}^{B}\right)

by time derivative (
5
) on scalar fields and decomposition (
10
).
With (
51
), (
48
) and (
41
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R},\partial_{k}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}

\displaystyle=g_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}kj}\partial_{t}r^{j}+u^{i}\left(\partial_{i}r_{k}-\Gamma_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}ik}^{j}r_{j}\right)+G_{ij}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}]r^{j}-\phi\left(u^{i}I\!I_{ik}+b_{k}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}]\right)

\displaystyle=g_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}kj}\partial_{t}r^{j}+u^{i}r_{k|i}+G_{ij}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}]r^{j}-\phi b_{k}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]=\left[\dot{\boldsymbol{r}}-\phi\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\right]_{k}\,\text{,}

where
\dot{\boldsymbol{r}}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}
is the material derivative of the tangential vector field
\boldsymbol{r}
given in table
4
.
For the normal part of
\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}
we use the time derivative (
5
) on scalar fields again and the rate of the normal field given in (
54
).
This yields

\displaystyle\left\langle\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R},\boldsymbol{\nu}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{AB}\dot{R}^{A}\nu^{B}=\dot{\overline{\left\langle\boldsymbol{R},\boldsymbol{\nu}\right\rangle}}_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}-R_{B}\dot{\nu}^{B}=\dot{\phi}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}}\,\text{.}

Corollary 1
.

For all
\boldsymbol{R}=\boldsymbol{r}+\phi\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
,
\boldsymbol{r}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}
and
\phi\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
holds

\displaystyle\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}

\displaystyle=\dot{\boldsymbol{r}}-\phi\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]+\left(\dot{\phi}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{r},\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}}\right)\boldsymbol{\nu}\,\text{.}

(11)

Note that
\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}
equals the material acceleration in an observer-invariant representation, see
[
6
,
22
]
.
To show inner product compatibility of the material derivative, we use that the proxy
\delta_{AB}
of the Cartesian metric tensor is in the kernel
of the scalar time derivative (
5
),
i. e. it holds
\dot{\delta}_{AB}=\partial_{t}\delta_{AB}+u^{k}\partial_{k}\delta_{AB}=0
.
Hence, we obtain
\dot{\overline{\left\langle\boldsymbol{R}_{1},\boldsymbol{R}_{2}\right\rangle}}_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}=\delta_{AB}(\dot{R}_{1}^{A}R_{2}^{B}+R_{1}^{A}\dot{R}_{2}^{B})

for all
\boldsymbol{R}_{1},\boldsymbol{R}_{2}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
, which gives the following corollary.

Corollary 2
.

The material derivative on vector fields is compatible with the inner product,
i. e. for all
\boldsymbol{R}_{1}=\boldsymbol{r}_{1}+\phi_{1}\boldsymbol{\nu},\boldsymbol{R}_{2}=\boldsymbol{r}_{2}+\phi_{2}\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
holds

\displaystyle\dot{\overline{\left\langle\boldsymbol{R}_{1},\boldsymbol{R}_{2}\right\rangle}}_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}_{1},\boldsymbol{R}_{2}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{R}_{1},\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}_{2}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}

(12)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\dot{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1},\boldsymbol{r}_{2}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{r}_{1},\dot{\boldsymbol{r}}_{2}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}}+\dot{\phi}_{1}\phi_{2}+\phi_{1}\dot{\phi}_{2}\,\text{.}

2.2.2
2-Tensor Fields

To represent the material derivative (
8
) on 2-tensor fields
\boldsymbol{R}
,
i. e.
\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}=\dot{R}^{AB}\boldsymbol{e}_{A}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{B}
, we use the orthogonal decomposition

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{R}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}^{T},\partial_{n}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}=\left[\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{R}+\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{r}-\phi\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\right]_{n}

as a consequence.
The pure normal part of the material derivative yields

\displaystyle\left\langle\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R},\boldsymbol{\nu}\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}

\displaystyle=\dot{R}^{AB}\delta_{AC}\delta_{BD}\nu^{C}\nu^{D}=\dot{\overline{\left\langle\boldsymbol{R},\boldsymbol{\nu}\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}\right\rangle}}_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}-R_{CD}\left(\dot{\nu}^{C}\nu^{D}+\nu^{C}\dot{\nu}^{D}\right)

\displaystyle=\dot{\phi}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta}_{L}+\boldsymbol{\eta}_{R},\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}}\,\text{.}

by (

) and (

).

Corollary 3
.

For all
\boldsymbol{R}=\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{\eta}_{L}\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}+\boldsymbol{\nu}\otimes\boldsymbol{\eta}_{R}+\phi\boldsymbol{\nu}\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
,

\boldsymbol{r}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}

holds

\displaystyle=\dot{\boldsymbol{r}}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{L}\otimes\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]-\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\boldsymbol{\eta}_{R}+\left(\dot{\phi}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta}_{L}+\boldsymbol{\eta}_{R},\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}}\right)\boldsymbol{\nu}\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}

(15)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\dot{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1},\boldsymbol{r}_{2}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{r}_{1},\dot{\boldsymbol{r}}_{2}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}}+\dot{\phi}_{1}\phi_{2}+\phi_{1}\dot{\phi}_{2}

valid, we obtain the following corollary.

,

\displaystyle=\dot{\boldsymbol{r}}\boldsymbol{p}+\boldsymbol{r}\dot{\boldsymbol{p}}+\dot{\psi}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{L}+\psi\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{L}-(\phi\psi+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta}_{R},\boldsymbol{p}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}})\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

2.3
Upper-Convected Derivative

In order to obtain the upper-convected derivative, we choose a pullback for the time derivative (
2
),
which adhere to the contravariant material proxy instead of the Cartesian proxy as it is stipulated for the material derivative.
We give the exact definition for the vector and tensor field case in its associated subsections.
In contrast to
[
6
]
, we use the short naming “upper-convected”
for “upper-upper-convected” or “fully-upper-convected”,
since we do not treat any mixed-convected derivative in this paper.

2.3.1
Vector Fields

We consider the upper-convected pullback
\Phi^{*_{\sharp}}_{t,\tau}:\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S},t+\tau}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S},t}

given by

\displaystyle(\Phi^{*_{\sharp}}_{t,\tau}\boldsymbol{R}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]|_{t+\tau})(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})

\displaystyle:=r^{i}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t+\tau,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})+\phi[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t+\tau,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})

for decompositions (
10
) of vector fields
\boldsymbol{R}
.
With this we define the upper-convected derivative by
\operatorname{D}^{\sharp}_{t}:=\operatorname{D}_{t}|_{\Phi^{*}_{t,\tau}=\Phi^{*_{\sharp}}_{t,\tau}}
,
i. e. the time derivative (
2
) yields

\displaystyle\operatorname{D}^{\sharp}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

\displaystyle=(\partial_{t}r^{i}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}])\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}+(\partial_{t}\phi[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}])\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

(17)

w. r. t. the material observer locally at material events
(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})
.
Instead of transforming the frame to an arbitrary observer frame, we simply relate this to the material derivative (
11
),
where we already know the observer-invariant description.
Term by term, we obtain

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}r^{i}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}])\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}(r^{A}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{e}_{A})-r^{i}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{r}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]-\boldsymbol{G}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{r}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]-\left\langle\boldsymbol{r}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}],\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}}\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\phi[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}])\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}(\phi[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}])-\phi[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]=\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}(\phi\boldsymbol{\nu})[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]+\phi[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

with (
48
) and (
53
).
The first summands are adding up to the material derivative
\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]
, which has an observer-invariant representation.
The remaining summands are instantaneous and hence are observer-invariant a priori.
Therefore we can express the upper-convected derivative by an arbitrary observer.
This justified to omit the parameterization argument
\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}
.
For instance, we write
\operatorname{D}^{\sharp}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}=\operatorname{D}^{\sharp}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{o}}}]
for short.
Moreover, we could relate
\dot{\boldsymbol{r}}
to the tangential upper-convected derivative by
\mathfrak{L}^{\sharp}\boldsymbol{r}=\dot{\boldsymbol{r}}-\boldsymbol{G}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{r}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}

given in table
4
.
We summarize this in the following corollary under the aid of the tensor field

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mathcal{G}}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

\displaystyle:=\boldsymbol{G}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]+\boldsymbol{\nu}\otimes\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]-\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}

(18)

\displaystyle\phantom{:}=\nabla\boldsymbol{v}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}-v_{\bot}\boldsymbol{I\!I}+\boldsymbol{\nu}\otimes\left(\nabla v_{\bot}+\boldsymbol{I\!I}\boldsymbol{v}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}\right)-\left(\nabla v_{\bot}+\boldsymbol{I\!I}\boldsymbol{v}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}\right)\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}\,\text{.}

Corollary 6
.

For all
\boldsymbol{R}=\boldsymbol{r}+\phi\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
,
\boldsymbol{r}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}
and
\phi\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
holds

\displaystyle\operatorname{D}^{\sharp}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{G}}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{R}=\mathfrak{L}^{\sharp}\boldsymbol{r}+\dot{\phi}\boldsymbol{\nu}\,\text{.}

(19)

Note that in contrast to the material or Jaumann derivative, the upper-convected derivative is not compatible with the inner product in general.
Substituting (
19
) into (
12
) yields

\displaystyle\dot{\overline{\left\langle\boldsymbol{R}_{1},\boldsymbol{R}_{2}\right\rangle}}_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\operatorname{D}^{\sharp}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}_{1},\boldsymbol{R}_{2}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{R}_{1},\operatorname{D}^{\sharp}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}_{2}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{G}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]+\boldsymbol{G}^{T}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}],\boldsymbol{R}_{1}\otimes\boldsymbol{R}_{2}\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40283pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}}

for all
\boldsymbol{R}_{1},\boldsymbol{R}_{2}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
,
where
\boldsymbol{G}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]+\boldsymbol{G}^{T}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]
is vanishing if and only if the material carries out a rigid body motion.

For extended vector fields
\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}_{h}
, which are sufficing
\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}|_{\xi=0}=\boldsymbol{R}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
,
we conclude from (
9
) and (
55
) that for the upper-convected
\mathbb{R}^{3}
-time derivative

\displaystyle\dot{\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}}-(\operatorname{\widehat{\nabla}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}})\widehat{\boldsymbol{R}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\operatorname{D}^{\sharp}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}

is valid for
h\rightarrow 0
.

2.3.2
2-Tensor Fields

We consider the upper-convected pullback
\Phi^{*_{\sharp}}_{t,\tau}:\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}^{2}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S},t+\tau}\rightarrow\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}^{2}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S},t}

given by

\displaystyle(\Phi^{*_{\sharp}}_{t,\tau}\boldsymbol{R}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]|_{t+\tau})(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})

\displaystyle:=r^{ij}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t+\tau,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})\otimes\partial_{j}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\eta_{L}^{i}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t+\tau,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\eta_{R}^{j}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t+\tau,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})\otimes\partial_{j}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\phi[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t+\tau,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}](t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})

for decompositions (
13
) of 2-tensor fields
\boldsymbol{R}
.
With this we define the upper-convected derivative by
\operatorname{D}^{\sharp}_{t}:=\operatorname{D}_{t}|_{\Phi^{*}_{t,\tau}=\Phi^{*_{\sharp}}_{t,\tau}}
,
i. e. the time derivative (
2
) yields

\displaystyle\operatorname{D}^{\sharp}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

\displaystyle=(\partial_{t}r^{ij}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}])\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes\partial_{j}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}+(\partial_{t}\phi[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}])\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+(\partial_{t}\eta_{L}^{i}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}])\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]+(\partial_{t}\eta_{R}^{j}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}])\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\partial_{j}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}

(20)

w. r. t. the material observer locally at material events
(t,y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{1},y_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}^{2})
.
Similar to the proceeding for vector fields, we just relate this to the material derivative.
Term by term, we obtain

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}r^{ij}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}])\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes\partial_{j}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\left(r^{AB}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{e}_{A}\otimes\boldsymbol{e}_{B}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-r^{ij}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\left(\mathchoice{G^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}}}}{G^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}}}}{G^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}}}}{G^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}}}}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\partial_{k}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}+b_{i}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\right)\otimes\partial_{j}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-r^{ij}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes\left(\mathchoice{G^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{G^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{G^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{G^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\partial_{k}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}+b_{j}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\right)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{r}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]-\boldsymbol{G}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{r}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]-\boldsymbol{r}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{G}^{T}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{r}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]-\boldsymbol{r}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\eta_{L}^{i}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}])\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{D}^{\sharp}_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{L}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]=\left(\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{L}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{G}}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{\eta}_{L}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\right)\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{L}\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu})[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]+\boldsymbol{\eta}_{L}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]-\boldsymbol{G}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{\eta}_{L}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta}_{L}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}],\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}}\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\eta_{R}^{j}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}])\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\partial_{j}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}

\displaystyle=\left((\partial_{t}\eta_{R}^{i}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}])\partial_{i}\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\right)^{T}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\nu}\otimes\boldsymbol{\eta}_{R})[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]+\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\boldsymbol{\eta}_{R}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]-\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\boldsymbol{G}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{\eta}_{R}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\left\langle\boldsymbol{\eta}_{R}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}],\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\right\rangle_{\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}}\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\phi[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}])\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

\displaystyle=\dot{\phi}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}(\phi\boldsymbol{\nu}\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu})[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]+\phi[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\left(\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]+\boldsymbol{\nu}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\otimes\boldsymbol{b}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}},\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\right)

due to (
48
), (
17
), (

) and (

).
The first summands are adding up to the material derivative
\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}[\boldsymbol{X}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]
, which has an observer-invariant representation.
The remaining summands are instantaneous and hence observer-invariant a priori.
Therefore we can express the upper-convected derivative by an arbitrary observer.
This justifies to omit observer arguments in square brackets.

Corollary 7
.

For all
\boldsymbol{R}=\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{\eta}_{L}\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}+\boldsymbol{\nu}\otimes\boldsymbol{\eta}_{R}+\phi\boldsymbol{\nu}\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{R}^{3}|_{\mathcal{S}}
,

\boldsymbol{r}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{2}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
,
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{L},\boldsymbol{\eta}_{R}\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}{\operatorname{T}\!}\mathcal{S}
and
\phi\in\mathchoice{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\operatorname{T}\!^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathcal{S}
holds

\displaystyle\operatorname{D}^{\sharp}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{D}^{\,{\!\mathfrak{m}}}_{t}\boldsymbol{R}-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{G}}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]\boldsymbol{R}-\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{G}}^{T}[\boldsymbol{V}_{{\!\mathfrak{m}}}]=\mathfrak{L}^{\sharp\sharp}\boldsymbol{r}+\mathfrak{L}^{\sharp}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{L}\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}+\boldsymbol{\nu}\otimes\mathfrak{L}^{\sharp}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{R}+\dot{\phi}\boldsymbol{\nu}\otimes\boldsymbol{\nu}\,\text{.}

(21)

Tangential upper-convected derivatives
\mathfrak{L}^{\sharp\sharp}
and
\mathfrak{L}^{\sharp}
on tangential tensor fields are given
in table
6
and
4
.
Note that in contrast to the material or Jaumann derivative, the upper-convected derivative is neither compatible with the inner nor the tensor product in general.
Substituting (

) into (
15
),
resp. (

) and (
19
) into (
